===PAGE 152=== (ocr, 40 lines)
   1|                    Substance abuse and the mental health setting job opportunities have increased with more funding being
   2|                    providea to combat the opiate crisis. Having a clinical rotation at AdCare Hospital in Worcester, and
   3|                    pertorming simulation scenarios involving substance abuse clients, has better prepared our graduates to
   4|                    work in a substance abuse clinical setting, as well.
   5|                    We are expecting an accreditation visit from the Massachusetts Board of Registration in Nursing this
   6|                    year. Our goals include stabilization of faculty positions, securing a certified Simulation Instructor, and pursuance of ACEN (Accreditation Commission for Education in Nursing) or other accreditation status
   7|                    to more accurately reflect the changing environment of nursing education.
   8|                    Looking Ahead
   9|                    While the Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School District educational community is
  10|                    certainly proud of the achievements of our talented students, faculty, and staff, we continue to have an eye
  11|                    toward the future, always committed to improving our vocational and academic programming,
  12|                    strengthening key partnerships, and maintaining facilities that contribute to student success and
  13|                    achievement. As we look ahead, there are a number of programs and initiatives that we expect will have
  14|                    a positive impact on our school and students for years to come.
  15|                    Expand Health Occupations program to include vital diagnostics: Monty Tech was awarded a generous
  16|                    $385,000 Skills Capital Grant, a portion of which will benefit the staff and students of the Health
  17|                    Uccupations program. To better prepare our graduates for a variety of careers in the health sector, the
  18|                    school will purchase new diagnostic equipment to train students in the proper procedures for testing for
  19|                    cholesterol, blood sugar, lead, lung volume, and more. In addition, the program will receive state-of-the
  20|                   -art virtual dissection technology, bringing lessons in anatomy and physiology to new heights. This
  21|                   equipment, provided by Anatomage and most often found on college campuses, is expected to engage
  22|                    students and bring   a deeper level of knowledge and understanding to students as they explore the
  23|                    human body and its complex systems.
  24|                    Celebrate the completion of an all-new Automotive Technology simulation lab: The $385,000 Skills
  25|                    Capital Grant award will also fund an all-new Automotive Technology simulation lab, which will
  26|                   include the latest technology in trainers and simulators aligned with NATEF standards.              At Monty
  27|                    Tech, students  are presented with countless opportunities to earn industry-recognized credentials.
  28|                    These certifications are not only a testament to the skills they have developed while enrolled at Monty
  29|                    Certifications to the program.     In partnership with Snap On Tools, Monty Tech will now present
  30|                    students with a more sophisticated technical training program and opportunities to earn all-new
  31|                    diagnostic credentials, validating their experience and ability to diagnose and service today's computer-
  32|                    controlled vehicles. In addition, the program will also receive new automotive lifts, floor jacks, engine
  33|                    stands, chargers and an air table, to ensure students are trained on equipment that will prepare them for
  34|                    careers in the automotive industry.
  35|                   Roll out affordable veterinary care services in the school's new veterinary clinic: A project more than 7
  36|                   years in the making, the Monty Tech Veterinary Clinic is now open, providing affordable veterinary
  37|                    care to pets across the region. The rollout of services continues throughout the 2019-2020 school year,
  38|                    with routine wellness care visits, affordable medications and vaccinations, diagnostic services, primary
  39|                    care surgical services, stable urgent care, compassionate end of life care, and dentistry.
  40|                                                                         150

   4|                   ARTICLE 41. To see whether the Town will vote to adopt a bylaw as follows:
   5|                   Special Event Permit Guidelines
   6|                    Welcome and Instructions The Town of Lunenburg is proud of the strong sense of
   7|                   community that exists amongst its residents. To further this sense of community the Town is
   8|                   happy to welcome special events from athletic events to concerts to community festivals. The
   9|                   goal of the Town of Lunenburg is to ensure that these events are safe and successful, while
  10|                   minimizing the impact on our residents and businesses. The following instructions are intended
  11|                   to assist applicants in planning and preparing to carry out their special event.
  12|                   Accordingly, the Selectmen shall be authorized to grant permits for special events, as defined
  13|                   in this bylaw, subject to the rules and regulations governing special events set forth herein.
  14|                   Guidelines for Special Events
  15|                   A Special Event is any private event/activity held on public property or an event open to the
  16|                   general public held on private property that involves or affects, directly or incidentally, the
  17|                   ordinary use of parks, playgrounds, fields, public buildings, streets and rights-of-way and/or
  18|                   sidewalks. Special events may include, but are not necessarily limited to, athletic tournaments,
  19|                   concerts, fairs, festivals, holiday celebrations, parades, road or bicycle races, trainings
  20|                   workshops. Properties may hold multiple Special Events, with a maximum of 6 events per
  21|                   private property, in a calendar year given that the appropriate permits, approvals and
  22|                   inspections are in place. Events beyond 6 not permitted without reference to zoning in the
  23|                   district.
  24|                   Individuals or organizations wishing to hold Special Events on public or private property must
  25|                   obtain a Special Event Permit from the Town of Lunenburg. Special Event Permit Applications
  26|                   must be filed a minimum of sixty (60) days prior to the event, failure to do so may result in the
  27|                   denial of application.
  28|                   Exemptions: The following events are exempt from Special Event Permitting:
  29|                   • Town Events held on Town property
  30|                   • Events held in conjunction with the Lunenburg Public Schools or the Lunenburg Public
  31|                        Library
  32|                   • Events held by Private Membership Organizations where only members and guests are
  33|                        invited
  34|                                                                         173

===PAGE 176=== (ocr, 35 lines)
   1|                   regulated and must be part of the permit.
   2|                   Special Events may span multiple days and include on-site camping (as permitted by the Board
   3|                   of Health) but shall not exceed 4 days. No more than Three (3) multiple day events may be
   4|                   approved per calendar year per private property.
   5|                   Alcohol / drug Use: Alcohol is prohibited in all Town of Lunenburg public property,
   6|                   including buildings, parks, playgrounds, fields, etc. Any event on private property may provide
   7|                   alcohol for a fee, provided that there is strict compliance with the Town of Lunenburg policy
   8|                   regarding such (Town of Lunenburg Board of Selectmen Policies and Procedures, as amended)
   9|                   No Bring Your Own Bottle (BYOB) Permits may be issued for Special Events. Private events
  10|                   that serve alcohol for free or allow participants to provide their own alcohol shall not be subject
  11|                   to this provision.
  12|                   Recreational use of drugs shall be prohibited at any permitted Special Event that takes place on
  13|                   Town of Lunenburg property. This shall be deemed to include marijuana.
  14|                   Restrooms/Trash/Cleanup: The Town of Lunenburg may require event organizers to
  15|                   provide temporary toilet facilities. Temporary toilet facilities must be cleaned daily or as
  16|                   necessary per the Lunenburg Board of Health. Trash must be disposed of in approved T of Heat to TH
  17|                   containers. Trash pickup and disposal is to be performed as required by the Board of Health.
  18|                   The applicant must clean the right-of-way or public property of all rubbish and debris, returning
  19|                   it to its pre-event condition at the conclusion of the event. If the applicant fails to clean up
  20|                   debris and trash within the specified period, cleanup will be arranged by the Town and charged
  21|                   to the applicant.
  22|                   Traffic & Parking: Parking is permitted in designated areas only. The event organizer
  23|                   will be responsible for providing a parking plan to the Lunenburg Police Department for their
  24|                   approval. Parking may not interfere with event entrances or emergency access.
  25|                   Traffic and Parking Plans shall not violate Chapter 332 of the Code of Lunenburg.
  26|                   Signs: Signs are permitted, though approval may be required by the Building Inspector.
  27|                   No signs may be affixed to trees, buildings or street furniture.
  28|                   Smoking: Smoking is not permitted in any public facility, on school grounds or on
  29|                   recreational parks, fields or playgrounds.
  30|                   Fireworks: fireworks are not permitted within the Town of Lunenburg without proper
  31|                   licensing.
  32|                   Tents: Large Function Tents over 2500 sq. feet require an inspection or permit from the
  33|                   Town of Lunenburg Board of Health, Building and Fire Departments. Such structures require a
  34|                   flame resistant certificate issues by an acceptable testing laboratory and site plan indicating a
  35|                                                                         174

===PAGE 177=== (ocr, 37 lines)
   1|                   documentation the Board of selectmen may reduce this distance at                    their aiscretion under
   2|                   reasonable circumstances.
   3|                   First Aid/Medical: Events may require provisions for first aid and medical personnel.
   4|                   Arrangements should be with a private service for the event. The Fire Department can provide a
   5|                   list of contacts.
   6|                   ACCESSABILITY: Any Special Event permitted through the Town of Lunenburg shall
   7|                   be accessible to all persons who may attend. This includes the provision of accessible parking
   8|                   spaces, paths, restroom facilities and any other reasonable accommodations that may be
   9|                   required.
  10|                   EVENT LAYOUT: All Special Event Applications shall include an Event Layout Plan
  11|                   that includes:
  12|                   Site Access
  13|                   Parking Areas
  14|                   Restroom Facilities
  15|                   Required Buffer Zone
  16|                   Location of any abutting houses
  17|                   Food Service Area (it applicable)
  18|                   Sleeping Accommodations (it available)
  19|                   Wetlands on the property (if applicable)
  20|                  First Aid Station (if applicable)
  21|                   Alcohol Serving Areas (if applicable)
  22|                   Location of Stage or Entertainment (if applicable)
  23|                   Route of Race (if applicable)
  24|                   Any Additional Event Features not listed.
  25|                   Insurance: At the discretion of the Board of Selectmen, all applicants must provide a
  26|                   certificate of insurance. Terms of insurance may be set by the Board of Selectmen in scope and
  27|                   financial amount at their discretion or set at the minimum amount of $1,000,000 for
  28|                   commercial general liability as a precondition for obtaining permits. The Certificate of
  29|                   Insurance must include all coverage deemed necessary for the event, as specified by the Town
  30|                   of Lunenburg and Town Counsel, including indemnification and hold harmless clause. The
  31|                   Certificate of Insurance must name the Town of Lunenburg as an additional insured on
  32|                   applicable policies. This Certificate and verification of Workers Compensation Coverage must
  33|                   be submitted to the Town Manager's Office no later than ten (10) business days prior to the
  34|                   date of the event. Final Special Event Permits will not be issued without submission of a
  35|                   Certificate of Insurance reflecting the necessary Workers Compensation Coverage to comply
  36|                   with State Law.
  37|                                                                         175

===PAGE 178=== (ocr, 39 lines)
   1|                                                                                                         U11 he souls ve hell
   2|                   applying event forgo any permit requirement in these guidelines.
   3|                   Enforcement: Town of Lunenburg Police, Fire or other staff so designated by the Town
   4|                   Manager, may request that a resident, event employee, or participant leave any park or public
   5|                   facility for violation of the rules and regulations.
   6|                   Special Event Permit Process:
   7|                   Filing of Special Event Permit Application & Supporting Material with Board of Selectmen
   8|                   Distribution of Material to Relevant Departments for Review
   9|                   Meeting of Relevant Departments to discuss event coordination
  10|                   Submission of Comments from Town Staff to BOS.
  11|                   BOS Public Hearing on Special Event Permit
  12|                   Vote on Special Event and Permit Issuance

  31|                    Code of Lunenburg, by deleting in its entirety Chapter 140 Article I, entitled
  32|                    "Excavations and Earth Removal," and substituting the language below therefor; or
  33|                    take any other action relative thereto.
  34|                                                                         181

===PAGE 184=== (ocr, 29 lines)
   1|                      § 140-2 Definitions.
   2|                       As used in this bylaw, the following terms shall have the meanings indicated:
   3|                       ABUTTERS The owners of property within 300 feet of the perimeter of the subject
   4|                      property.
   5|                       APPLICANT The owner of land from which earth will be removed or fill to be placed,
   6|                       or the person of lawful standing in the stead of such owner. Any person, company, or agent
   7|                      standing instead of the actual property owner must have the owner's written permission to
   8|                       do so.
   9|                      BOARD The Planning Board of the Town of Lunenburg, which shall act as the permit
  10|                      granting authority (PGA).
  11|                      EARTH This term shall include soil, loam, sand, gravel, clay, peat, rock, or other
  12|                      earth material in solid form.
  13|                      EARTH REMOVAL OPERATION Any commercial mining, stripping, quarrying,
  14|                      tilling, digging or blasting of earth originating trom the lown of Lunenburg and its
  15|                      transportation into or out of the Town of Lunenburg.
  16|                      EROSION The process by which the ground surface is worn by forces such as wind,
  17|                      running water, ice, abrasion, gravity, transportation or by artificial means.
  18|                      FILLING OF LAND The import and use of earth materials on any parcel of land
  19|                      within the Town of Lunenburg.
  20|                      GRAVEL Loose fragments of rock or coarse aggregate resulting from natural
  21|                      disintegration and abrasion of rock or processing of weakly bound conglomerate.
  22|                      LOAM OR TOPSOIL A soil consisting of a friable mixture of varying proportions of
  23|                      clay, sand, silt, and organic matter.
  24|                      PGA The permit granting authority, which will be the Planning Board for the
  25|                      purposes of this article.
  26|                      PROCESSING The sorting or separation of earth materials into distinct categories
  27|                      based on particle size or type usually through the use of a screening process, not including
  28|                      stone crushing.
  29|                                                                         182

===PAGE 185=== (ocr, 26 lines)
   1|                      less in diameter based on the Wentworth scale of measurement.
   2|                       SITE A distinct portion of one or more contiguous lots, under the same or different
   3|                       ownerships, on which an earth removal and/or fill operation is conducted, or is proposed to
   4|                       be conducted, under the permit.
   5|                       SLOPE An area that is more or less steep, as measured by the vertical rise over the
   6|                      horizontal distance, expressed as a percentage or ratio. For example, a rise of one foot over
   7|                      three horizontal feet is a slope of 33% and is expressed as a ratio of 3:1 slope.
   8|                       STONE CRUSHING The mechanical operation which creates smaller-sized stones or
   9|                      stone products from larger-sized stones, boulders or particles typically using a crushing
  10|                       plant or similar machinery.
  11|                       § 140-3 Applicability.
  12|                       A. All earth removal and earth filling operations in the Town shall provide the following
  13|                      information, in writing, to the Building Inspector within six months of the effective date of
  14|                      this article:
  15|                      (1) The map and parcel number of the subject property;
  16|                      (2) An estimate of the amount of material left to be removed (it earth removal);
  17|                      (3) An estimate of the amount of fill material remaining to be placed (it fill);
  18|                       (4) An estimate of the anticipated annual volume of activity;
  19|                       (5) A description of completed and planned reclamation of the property;
  20|                       (6) The date the operation began; and
  21|                       (7) The anticipated date of completion or cessation of the operations.
  22|                       B. Except as provided in § 140-4 or 140-5, a permit shall be required for any of the
  23|                           following activities:
  24|                       (1) Earth removal that involves 500 cubic yards or more of material per calendar year
  25|                           (January through December).
  26|                                                                        183

===PAGE 186=== (ocr, 29 lines)
   1|                       C. An earth removal or filling operation that does not exceed any of the above thresholds
   2|                       shall not require a permit but is subject to the following requirements (unless otherwise
   3|                       agreed to, in writing, by the abutting property owner):
   4|                       (1) The operation shall not encroach closer than 10 feet to a property line; and
   5|                       (2) No greater than a 1:1 slope shall exist between the operation and the ten-foot buffer.
   6|                       § 140-4 Exemptions.
   7|                       The following earth removal or fill operations are exempted from the requirement to obtain
   8|                       a permit under this article:
   9|                       A. Earth removal or the placement of fill associated with the installation of septic systems,
  10|                           which shall be governed by the Commonwealth of Massachusetts Environmental Code
  11|                           (Title 5, 310 CMR 15.000).
  12|                       B. Earth removal or the placement of fill associated with the installation of foundations
  13|                       for new buildings and/or building additions, which shall be governed by MGL c. 143 and
  14|                      the Commonwealth of Massachusetts Building Code (780 CMR).
  15|                       C. Earth removal or the placement of fill proposed for land falling within the Town's
  16|                       Floodplain Protection District, which shall be governed by §§ 250-4.8 of the Lunenburg
  17|                       Zoning Bylaw and require a special permit from the Planning Board.
  18|                       D. Earth removal or the placement of fill proposed for land falling within the Town's
  19|                       Wetland Protection Bylaw and associated buffer zones, which shall be governed by the
  20|                       Wetlands Chapter Within the Town's General Bylaws (Chapter 239), which is administered
  21|                      by the Conservation Commission, and the Massachusetts Wetlands Protection Act.
  22|                       E. Earth removal or the placement of fill that involves less than 500 cubic yards.
  23|                       t. Earth removal or the placement of fill, where the operation occurs entirely within an
  24|                           individual parcel and where a Town-accepted public way is not used tor the
  25|                           transportation of the material.
  26|                       G. Earth removal or the placement of fill within the right-of-way for a new subdivision
  27|                      road that has been approved by the Planning Board or Zoning Board where there is already
  28|                      a bond in place with the Town to ensure performance.
  29|                                                                         184

===PAGE 187=== (ocr, 31 lines)
   1|                      deposited  on adjacent properties; or to otherwise cause nulsances,                   hazaras,    or olner
   2|                      objectionable conditions detrimental to health, safety, or property values in adjacent areas.
   3|                      ili. the excavation is not active for a period of time greater than is reasonable for the activity
   4|                      being conducted.
   5|                     iv. Any earth removal or fill is limited to the necessary scope of the project.
   6|                      The PGA shall, upon petition by an abutter or by any Town Official or Town Board, review
   7|                      an operation that would be exempt from the permit requirement pursuant to this § 140-4 and
   8|                      may determine that, because of the nature and scope of the earth removal or fill operation, a
   9|                      permit is nonetheless required.
  10|                      § 140-5 Continuation of Existing soil removal operations.
  11|                      A. An existing operation that had been issued a permit by the Board of Selectmen under
  12|                      the Earth Removal Bylaw that was in effect prior to the effective date of this Bylaw may
  13|                      continue to operate according to the terms of such permit, which shall hereafter be
  14|                      enforceable by the PGA pursuant to this article.
  15|                      B. An existing earth removal and/or fill operation that was not required to obtain a permit
  16|                      under the prior Earth Removal Bylaw, but which exceeds the thresholds specified in § 140-
  17|                      3B of this article, may continue to operate without being required to apply for or obtain a
  18|                      permit for a period of three years from the effective date of this article, provided that the
  19|                      owner or operator of said operation submits to the Building Inspector the information
  20|                      required in § 140-3A within six months of the effective date of this article.
  21|                      C. The right of an existing operation to operate pursuant to § 140-5A under a permit
  22|                      previously issued by the Board of Selectmen, or to operate without a permit pursuant to
  23|                      § 140-5B, shall expire if the operation is discontinued. An operation shall be deemed to have
  24|                      been discontinued if no earth removal or fill activity occurs at the site for a period of 12
  25|                      consecutive months.
  26|                      § 140-6 Permit criteria for earth removal and fill operations.
  27|                      A. Permits for earth removal and/or fill operations shall be granted by the PGA only upon
  28|                      its written determination that the proposed use shall not cause substantial detriment to the
  29|                      neighborhood, or the Town, taking into account the characteristics of the site and the
  30|                      proposal in relation to the site and surrounding environment.
  31|                                                                         185

===PAGE 188=== (ocr, 32 lines)
   1|                    (4) Impacts on the natural environment; and
   2|                   (5) Potential fiscal impact, including impact on Town services, tax base and property
   3|                         values.
   4|                    B. Determination for each of the five criteria shall be set forth in the permit decision as findings
   5|                   of fact.
   6|                    § 140-7 General limitations.
   7|                    A. No permit shall be issued for the removal of earth or the placement of fill in any location if
   8|                    such an operation:
   9|                   (1) Will endanger the public safety, public health or constitute a nuisance.
  10|                   (2) Will produce noise, dust, or other noxious effects observable at the lot lines of the property
  11|                   in amounts objectionable or detrimental to the normal use of adjacent properties.
  12|                    (3) Will result in the transportation of materials in such manner as to cause traffic congestion,
  13|                   dust, spillage, noise, or other nuisances or hazards, particularly on residential streets.
  14|                   (4) Will result in the transportation over ways which will be unduly injured thereby.
  15|                   4) i real in eage rape ply as over i i distrangeon to the most
  16|                   (6) Will cause irreparable harm to or loss of important wildlife, wildlife habitat or rare plant
  17|                   species indigenous to the area.
  18|                   (7) Will result in stormwater damaging abutting properties.
  19|                   B. Applicants are encouraged to submit a written statement regarding how each of the previous
  20|                   items will be addressed and/or mitigated.
  21|                   § 140-8 Regulations.
  22|                   The PGA may adopt and periodically amend rules and regulations for the implementation of
  23|                   this article, by majority vote. Such rules and regulations may set forth performance standards
  24|                   for earth removal and fill operations, impose filing and consultant fees, define additional terms
  25|                   not inconsistent with the article, and establish administrative procedures. Failure by the PGA to
  26|                   adopt such rules and regulations or a legal declaration of their invalidity by a court of law shall
  27|                   not act to suspend or invalidate the effect of this article.
  28|                   § 140-9 Permit procedures.
  29|                       A. Any person wishing to obtain a permit to remove from or place earth material on a
  30|                      property in the Town, or to use any public way within the Town for transporting such
  31|                      material, shall file a completed application for a permit together with any required
  32|                                                                         186

===PAGE 189=== (ocr, 36 lines)
   1|                       PGA after evidence is presented that all conditions of the expiring permit have been
   2|                       complied with and the work has been performed in good faith. There is no limit on the
   3|                       number of permit renewals an applicant can apply for. A public hearing may be required by
   4|                       the PGA, at its discretion, for renewal of permits.
   5|                       § 140-10 Public hearing.
   6|                       A. Once a complete permit application is filed with the PGA, the PGA shall set a date for a
   7|                       public hearing and so notify the applicant. Notice of the hearing shall be published by the
   8|                       PGA, at the applicant's expense, in a newspaper of general circulation in each of two
   9|                       successive weeks, the first publication being not less than 14 days before the day of said
  10|                       hearing. Notice of the hearing shall be given to all owners of real estate abutting upon the
  11|                       land specified in the permit application or lying within 300 feet of the subject parcel, all as
  12|                       appearing on the most recent tax list. The PGA will arrange for the publication and
  13|                       transmission of the notice of the hearing to the abutters, and the costs will be billed to the
  14|                       applicant. Final approval for the permit shall not be made until all hearing fees have been
  15|                       paid in full.
  16|                        B. The PGA's final action, rendered in writing, shall consist of either:
  17|                             1) Approval of the site plan based upon determination that the proposed plan will
  18|                                 constitute a suitable development and is in compliance with the standards set forth
  19|                                 in this Bylaw; or
  20|                             2) Disapproval of the site plan based upon a determination that the proposed plan does
  21|                             not meet the standards set forth in this Bylaw; or
  22|                             3) Approval of the site plan subject to any condition, modification or restriction
  23|                             required by the Planning Board which will ensure that the project meets the standards
  24|                             set forth in this Bylaw.
  25|                       § 140-11 Bond or security.
  26|                       The Board may require suitable bond or other security adequate to assure compliance with
  27|                       any of the provisions of this bylaw.
  28|                        § 140-12 Enforcement; violations and penalties.
  29|                        A. The PGA or its authorized agent shall enforce this article, its regulations, and the
  30|                       requirements and conditions of permits issued thereunder and may pursue civil and criminal
  31|                       remedies for violations of the same.
  32|                        B. The Building Inspector is authorized to conduct inspections on behalf of the PGA. To
  33|                       the extent permitted by law, or if authorized by the owner, operator, or other person in
  34|                        control of the site, the Building Inspector or other agent of the PGA may enter on the site to
  35|                        conduct inspections.
  36|                                                                         187

  28|                     privileges and restrictions pertaining thereto, to the licensing authority and the licensing
